ParlaMint-NO_2012-03-01.ana.ud2619e3711	As the rapporteur has explained, it is therefore a matter of whether the fuel GTL should drop the mineral oil tax. The fuel charging system is the subject of a debate we have every year, not least in the context of the treatment of the State budget. At the same time, we know that sometimes there is disagreement between customs and charging authorities and others about products to be covered by definitions that will then be charged. In this case, there is such a disagreement between EcoFuel that delivers the GTL fuel and the customs and charging authorities. The case shall now be tried before the Court, as the Advocate-General has said. Regulatory definition of products to be subject to charging has been granted to the charging authorities. When this case is now to be tried before the court, it falls unnatural for us to make a decision in a single case. We have also explained this in the committee position. But I would like to add that it is unnatural for us to conduct individual proceedings regardless of whether the case is going to court, and in that way ask the government to base a certain understanding of the rules. In the process of dealing with this matter, there has, after all, been good correspondence between several parties at Stortinget and the government. If we allow exemptions for mineral oil charges in individual cases, we would still be challenged at: Where is the limit going? Already today, we see that we are challenged by both definitions of the fuels to be covered by today's legislation and border crossings when a fuel is so clean that it should be exempted or included in today's legislation. Thus, we will continue to be challenged by changing current legislation to adapt to new types of fuels. It's only a few months ago, i.e. during the treatment of the state budget for 2012, the Storting signaled that we want fewer and no more exemptions from the tax, so that by 2020 we can have a user charge for all fuels according to their energy value. We want this because we want to make it clear that there are costs related to driving on the road – in the form of accidents, queues, noise, road wear and emissions harmful to health and the environment. Of course, these costs must be covered. Those who charge the costs must be involved in covering them. However, it is that our charging system is not perfect. Until then, user charges vary too much. Therefore, it is both important and right that we introduce an user charge by 2020 for all fuel types. Therefore, it is completely wrong, in our opinion, to go the opposite way and introduce new exceptions now. With this in mind, we do not support the proposal for a representative.
ParlaMint-NO_2012-03-01.ana.ud2619e3728	GTL is an environmental fuel made of pure natural gas. This environmental fuel can cause significantly lower emissions of both NOx, particulate matter, carbon dioxide and carbon monoxide. The natural gas from which the GTL is made is also an environmental fuel. Therefore, there is no so-called mineral oil tax on natural gas — or other so-called user charges, for that matter. For the drivers, however, natural gas is a major disadvantage, and it is that the cars have to be rebuilt. The ingenious feature of the GTL is that the natural gas has been transformed so that it is liquid at normal atmospheric pressure. That means you can use GTL in normal diesel cars. So the GTL is a smart way to get normal drivers to use natural gas – without having to rebuild the cars. Since natural gas is exempt from user charges, it might be thought that the GTL, which is made of 100 pst. pure natural gas is also exempt from user charges, such as: mineral oil levy. GTL is, after all, chemically free from mineral oil, as confirmed by the Customs Directorate’s own laboratory. However, the working party, SV and the Centre like incomprehensible and twisted rules that hit people the most, and are therefore in favour of a mineral oil tax on a fuel that is neither mineral nor made of oil. In the Minister of Finance's reply to the proposal for a representative, we find the following strange reason for the levy on the GTL: ‘The fact that a product is made from non-taxable raw materials does not therefore mean that the final product is the same.’ The fact that it is also possible to make synthetic diesel from coal is an unusually poor argument in this respect. No one has claimed that coal is an environmentally friendly fuel for the Norwegian car park. I have noticed that the members of the government of the financial committee in the vote argue that there should be a general user charge on all fuels, including those fuels that are currently exempted. This clearly shows that government parties do not take local pollution in cities seriously, but that the opposite is true of the state getting the most money. I wish that the Workers' Party, SV and the Centre had a little more empathy with all those who suffer asthma and airway plagues from government policies, where everything is about CO2 and about as much money as possible to the state treasury, and where local pollution and people's health are obviously of secondary importance. Self-taught environmental parties such as SV have nothing at all to boast about environmentally friendly transport. If the Progress Party had got through on its policy, most people would have had newer and more environmentally friendly cars, and environmental fuels would have been much more reasonable than today. In addition, we had environmentally friendly and traffic-safe roads across the country, where people could have driven at regular speeds and with correspondingly low consumption. We also remember what the government parties did to sabotage biodiesel in Norway. Exempting GTL from mineral oil taxes is one of the most important things we can do to limit local pollution in Norway. I urge all those who take severe asthma and respiratory disease to vote in favour of the proposal.

ParlaMint-NO_2012-03-01.ana.ud2619e4479	Alternatively, between the Committee's position and the proposals No. 1-3 from the Party of Advancement, Right and Christian People's Party. Proposal No. 1 sounds: ‘The government is asking the government to establish a more balanced management of healthcare by placing the mission documents and governance dialogue as much emphasis on concrete objectives on patient safety and quality as on economic and other governance parameters that the annual supervisory message of the State Health Authority and the country-wide annual reports of the Patient and User Ombudsmen are transformed into a quality and patient safety report based on the model of the Annual Communications of the Data Protection Authority and the Subject of Data Protection that is treated as a wholesale notification on an annual basis that the National Health Authority’s national supervisory notices on different issues of primary health services are to be addressed to the local government that quality, patient safety and working environment are to be a fixed theme in corporate meetings and at board meetings of health establishments. The quality selection of hospitals shall have a clearer and more active role.’ 2 sounds: ‘The government is asked to establish clear and national quality systems in healthcare through: the development of a national system for the documentation and visualisation of quality, learning and quality improvement with good national quality indicators that can be used for the management of the activity, following a model from Denmark that patient-experienced quality should be given equal weight to other quality indicators. Provision shall be made for specialist healthcare and caregivers to acquire knowledge of patients and their relatives' experience and use it in the quality improvement work. a selection of indicators of patient-experienced quality shall be published as a basis for patients’ choice of services to establish standard internal health care control systems. These must be based on experience from other risk areas, such as aviation, petroleum and food activities. the establishment of a number of medical quality records and a greater focus on better technological solutions that enhance the quality of the registers that all health establishments should be ISO certified by 2013.’;» Proposal No. 3 sounds: ‘The government is asked to work towards the establishment of a safety-focused and learning culture in healthcare through: health care develops guidelines on how to meet patients and their relatives after adverse events. It is also necessary to provide guidance on how health professionals involved are to be cared for. that patients/patients are given the status of a party to the supervision cases dealt with by the Health Authority and thus have the right to appeal to the decisions of the Health Authority that the police are sufficiently competent to investigate matters concerning adverse health incidents that the request for the assessment of an offence shall also concern system failures. The health service is required to inform patients/patients about what has happened, why it has happened, and what is done to prevent something similar from happening again. that the notification under the Special Health Service Act § 3-3 is to go to the State Health Authority before they proceed to the National Knowledge Centre for anonymised health services that a notification system for deviations in the community health service is established that the interpretation of the obligation of professional secrecy does not prevent learning processes in the ambulance service.» The left has announced support for the proposals.
